Section 383.3081, Florida Statutes, is created 1223 to read: 1224 383.3081 Advanced birth center designation.— 1225 (1) To be designated as an advanced birth center, a birth 1226 center must, in addition to maintaining compliance with all of 1227 the requirements under ss. 383.30-383.332 applicable to birth 1228 centers and advanced birth centers, meet all of the following 1229 criteria: 1230 (a) Be operated and staffed 24 hours per day, 7 days per 1231 week. 1232 (b) Employ two medical directors to oversee the activities 1233 of the center, one of whom must be a board-certified 1234 obstetrician and one of whom must be a board-certified 1235 anesthesiologist. 1236 (c) Have at least one properly equipped, dedicated surgical 1237 suite for the performance of cesarean deliveries. 1238 (d) Employ at least one registered nurse and ensure that at 1239 least one registered nurse is present in the center at all times 1240 and has the ability to stabilize and facilitate the transfer of 1241 patients and newborn infants when appropriate. 1242 (e) Enter into a written agreement with a blood bank for 1243 emergency blood bank services and have written protocols for the 1244 management of obstetrical hemorrhage which include provisions 1245 for emergency blood transfusions. Section 409.91256, Florida Statutes, is created 2155 to read: 2156 409.91256 Training, Education, and Clinicals in Health 2157 (TEACH) Funding Program.— 2158 (1) PURPOSE AND INTENT.—The Training, Education, and 2159 Clinicals in Health (TEACH) Funding Program is created to 2160 provide a high-quality educational experience while supporting 2161 participating federally qualified health centers, community 2162 mental health centers, rural health clinics, and certified 2163 community behavioral health clinics by offsetting administrative 2164 costs and loss of revenue associated with training residents and 2165 students to become licensed health care practitioners. Section 456.4501, Florida Statutes, is created 3479 to read: 3480 ENROLLED 2024 Legislature CS for SB 7016, 1st Engrossed 20247016er Page 121 of 232 CODING: Words stricken are deletions; words underlined are additions. 456.4501 Interstate Medical Licensure Compact.—The 3481 Interstate Medical Licensure Compact is hereby enacted into law 3482 and entered into by this state with all other jurisdictions 3483 legally joining therein in the form substantially as follows: 3484 SECTION 1 3486 PURPOSE 3487 In order to strengthen access to health care, and in 3488 recognition of the advances in the delivery of health care, the 3489 member states of the Interstate Medical Licensure Compact have 3490 allied in common purpose to develop a comprehensive process that 3491 complements the existing licensing and regulatory authority of 3492 state medical boards and provides a streamlined process that 3493 allows physicians to become licensed in multiple states, thereby 3494 enhancing the portability of a medical license and ensuring the 3495 safety of patients. The compact creates another pathway for 3496 licensure and does not otherwise change a state’s existing 3497 medical practice act. The compact also adopts the prevailing 3498 standard for licensure and affirms that the practice of medicine 3499 occurs where the patient is located at the time of the 3500 physician-patient encounter and, therefore, requires the 3501 physician to be under the jurisdiction of the state medical 3502 board where the patient is located. State medical boards that 3503 participate in the compact retain the jurisdiction to impose an 3504 adverse action against a license to practice medicine in that 3505 state issued to a physician through the procedures in the 3506 compact. 3507 SECTION 2 3509 ENROLLED 2024 Legislature CS for SB 7016, 1st Engrossed 20247016er Page 122 of 232 CODING: Words stricken are deletions; words underlined are additions. DEFINITIONS 3510 As used in the compact, the term: 3511 (1) “Bylaws” means those bylaws established by the 3512 Interstate Commission pursuant to Section 11 for its governance 3513 or for directing and controlling its actions and conduct. 3514 (2) “Commissioner” means the voting representative 3515 appointed by each member board pursuant to Section 11. 3516 (3) “Conviction” means a finding by a court that an 3517 individual is guilty of a criminal offense, through adjudication 3518 or entry of a plea of guilt or no contest to the charge by the 3519 offender. Evidence of an entry of a conviction of a criminal 3520 offense by the court shall be considered final for purposes of 3521 disciplinary action by a member board. 3522 (4) “Expedited license” means a full and unrestricted 3523 medical license granted by a member state to an eligible 3524 physician through the process set forth in the compact. 3525 (5) “Interstate Commission” means the Interstate Medical 3526 Licensure Compact Commission created pursuant to Section 11. 3527 (6) “License” means authorization by a state for a 3528 physician to engage in the practice of medicine, which would be 3529 unlawful without the authorization. 3530 (7) “Medical practice act” means laws and regulations 3531 governing the practice of allopathic and osteopathic medicine 3532 within a member state. 3533 (8) “Member board” means a state agency in a member state 3534 which acts in the sovereign interests of the state by protecting 3535 the public through licensure, regulation, and education of 3536 physicians as directed by the state government. 3537 (9) “Member state” means a state that has enacted the 3538 ENROLLED 2024 Legislature CS for SB 7016, 1st Engrossed 20247016er Page 123 of 232 CODING: Words stricken are deletions; words underlined are additions. compact. 3539 (10) “Offense” means a felony, high court misdemeanor, or 3540 crime of moral turpitude. 3541 (11) “Physician” means any person who: 3542 (a) Is a graduate of a medical school accredited by the 3543 Liaison Committee on Medical Education, the Commission on 3544 Osteopathic College Accreditation, or a medical school listed in 3545 the International Medical Education Directory or its equivalent; 3546 (b) Passed each component of the United States Medical 3547 Licensing Examination (USMLE) or the Comprehensive Osteopathic 3548 Medical Licensing Examination (COMLEX-USA) within three 3549 attempts, or any of its predecessor examinations accepted by a 3550 state medical board as an equivalent examination for licensure 3551 purposes; 3552 (c) Successfully completed graduate medical education 3553 approved by the Accreditation Council for Graduate Medical 3554 Education or the American Osteopathic Association; 3555 (d) Holds specialty certification or a time-unlimited 3556 specialty certificate recognized by the American Board of 3557 Medical Specialties or the American Osteopathic Association’s 3558 Bureau of Osteopathic Specialists; words underlined are additions. jurisdiction; 3568 (g) Has never held a license authorizing the practice of 3569 medicine subjected to discipline by a licensing agency in any 3570 state, federal, or foreign jurisdiction, excluding any action 3571 related to nonpayment of fees related to a license; 3572 (h) Has never had a controlled substance license or permit 3573 suspended or revoked by a state or the United States Drug 3574 Enforcement Administration; and 3575 (i) Is not under active investigation by a licensing agency 3576 or law enforcement authority in any state, federal, or foreign 3577 jurisdiction. 3578 (12) “Practice of medicine” means the diagnosis, treatment, 3579 prevention, cure, or relieving of a human disease, ailment, 3580 defect, complaint, or other physical or mental condition by 3581 attendance, advice, device, diagnostic test, or other means, or 3582 offering, undertaking, attempting to do, or holding oneself out 3583 as able to do any of these acts. 3584 (13) “Rule” means a written statement by the Interstate 3585 Commission adopted pursuant to Section 12 of the compact which 3586 is of general applicability; words underlined are additions. state of residence for purpose of federal income tax. 3626 (2) A physician may redesignate a member state as state of 3627 principal license at any time, as long as the state meets one of 3628 the descriptions under subsection (1). 3629 (3) The Interstate Commission may develop rules to 3630 facilitate redesignation of another member state as the state of 3631 principal license. 3632 SECTION 5 3634 APPLICATION AND ISSUANCE OF EXPEDITED LICENSURE 3635 (1) A physician seeking licensure through the compact must 3636 file an application for an expedited license with the member 3637 board of the state selected by the physician as the state of 3638 principal license. 3639 (2) Upon receipt of an application for an expedited 3640 license, the member board within the state selected as the state 3641 of principal license shall evaluate whether the physician is 3642 eligible for expedited licensure and issue a letter of 3643 qualification, verifying or denying the physician’s eligibility, 3644 to the Interstate Commission. 3645 (a) Static qualifications, which include verification of 3646 medical education, graduate medical education, results of any 3647 medical or licensing examination, and other qualifications as 3648 determined by the Interstate Commission through rule, are not 3649 subject to additional primary source verification if already 3650 primary source-verified by the state of principal license. 3651 (b) The member board within the state selected as the state 3652 of principal license shall, in the course of verifying 3653 eligibility, perform a criminal background check of an 3654 ENROLLED 2024 Legislature CS for SB 7016, 1st Engrossed 20247016er Page 127 of 232 CODING: Words stricken are deletions; This 3670 license authorizes the physician to practice medicine in the 3671 issuing state consistent with the medical practice act and all 3672 applicable laws and regulations of the issuing member board and 3673 member state. 3674 (5) An expedited license is valid for a period consistent 3675 with the licensure period in the member state and in the same 3676 manner as required for other physicians holding a full and 3677 unrestricted license within the member state. 3678 (6) An expedited license obtained through the compact must 3679 be terminated if a physician fails to maintain a license in the 3680 state of principal license for a nondisciplinary reason, without 3681 redesignation of a new state of principal license. 3682 (7) The Interstate Commission may develop rules regarding 3683 ENROLLED 2024 Legislature CS for SB 7016, 1st Engrossed 20247016er Page 128 of 232 CODING: Words stricken are deletions; INTERSTATE MEDICAL LICENSURE COMPACT COMMISSION 3800 (1) The member states hereby create the Interstate Medical 3801 Licensure Compact Commission. 3802 (2) The purpose of the Interstate Commission is the 3803 administration of the compact, which is a discretionary state 3804 function. 3805 (3) The Interstate Commission is a body corporate and joint 3806 agency of the member states and has all the responsibilities, 3807 powers, and duties set forth in the compact, and such additional 3808 powers as may be conferred upon it by a subsequent concurrent 3809 action of the respective legislatures of the member states in 3810 accordance with the terms of the compact. 3811 (4) The Interstate Commission shall consist of two voting 3812 representatives appointed by each member state, who shall serve 3813 as commissioners. In states where allopathic and osteopathic 3814 physicians are regulated by separate member boards, or if the 3815 licensing and disciplinary authority is split between multiple 3816 member boards within a member state, the member state shall 3817 appoint one representative from each member board. Each 3818 commissioner must be one of the following: 3819 (a) An allopathic or osteopathic physician appointed to a 3820 member board. 3821 (b) An executive director, an executive secretary, or a 3822 similar executive of a member board. 3823 (c) A member of the public appointed to a member board. 3824 (5) The Interstate Commission shall meet at least once each 3825 calendar year. A portion of this meeting must be a business 3826 meeting to address such matters as may properly come before the 3827 commission, including the election of officers. The chairperson 3828 ENROLLED 2024 Legislature CS for SB 7016, 1st Engrossed 20247016er Page 133 of 232 CODING: Words stricken are deletions; words underlined are additions. may call additional meetings and shall call for a meeting upon 3829 the request of a majority of the member states. 3830 (6) The bylaws may provide for meetings of the Interstate 3831 Commission to be conducted by telecommunication or other 3832 electronic means. 3833 (7) Each commissioner participating at a meeting of the 3834 Interstate Commission is entitled to one vote. A majority of 3835 commissioners constitutes a quorum for the transaction of 3836 business, unless a larger quorum is required by the bylaws of 3837 the Interstate Commission. A commissioner may not delegate a 3838 vote to another commissioner. In the absence of its 3839 commissioner, a member state may delegate voting authority for a 3840 specified meeting to another person from that state who must 3841 meet the qualification requirements specified in subsection (4). 3842 (8) The Interstate Commission shall provide public notice 3843 of all meetings, and all meetings must be open to the public. 3844 The Interstate Commission may close a meeting, in full or in 3845 portion, where it determines by a two-thirds vote of the 3846 commissioners present that an open meeting would be likely to: 3847 (a) Relate solely to the internal personnel practices and 3848 procedures of the Interstate Commission; 3849 (b) Discuss matters specifically exempted from disclosure 3850 by federal statute; 3851 (c) Discuss trade secrets or commercial or financial 3852 information that is privileged or confidential; 3853 (d) Involve accusing a person of a crime, or formally 3854 censuring a person; 3855 (e) Discuss information of a personal nature, the 3856 disclosure of which would constitute a clearly unwarranted 3857 ENROLLED 2024 Legislature CS for SB 7016, 1st Engrossed 20247016er Page 134 of 232 CODING: Words stricken are deletions; words underlined are additions. invasion of personal privacy; 3858 (f) Discuss investigative records compiled for law 3859 enforcement purposes; or 3860 (g) Specifically relate to participation in a civil action 3861 or other legal proceeding. 3862 (9) The Interstate Commission shall keep minutes that fully 3863 describe all matters discussed in a meeting and provide a full 3864 and accurate summary of actions taken, including a record of any 3865 roll call votes. 3866 (10) The Interstate Commission shall make its information 3867 and official records, to the extent not otherwise designated in 3868 the compact or by its rules, available to the public for 3869 inspection. 3870 (11) The Interstate Commission shall establish an executive 3871 committee, which shall include officers, members, and others as 3872 determined by the bylaws. The executive committee has the power 3873 to act on behalf of the Interstate Commission, with the 3874 exception of rulemaking, during periods when the Interstate 3875 Commission is not in session. When acting on behalf of the 3876 Interstate Commission, the executive committee shall oversee the 3877 administration of the compact, including enforcement and 3878 compliance with the compact and its bylaws and rules, and other 3879 duties as necessary. 3880 (12) The Interstate Commission may establish other 3881 committees for governance and administration of the compact. 3882 SECTION 11 3884 POWERS AND DUTIES OF THE INTERSTATE COMMISSION 3885 The Interstate Commission has all of the following powers 3886 ENROLLED 2024 Legislature CS for SB 7016, 1st Engrossed 20247016er Page 135 of 232 CODING: Words stricken are deletions; FINANCE POWERS 3945 (1) The Interstate Commission may levy on and collect an 3946 annual assessment from each member state to cover the cost of 3947 the operations and activities of the Interstate Commission and 3948 its staff. The total assessment, subject to appropriation, must 3949 be sufficient to cover the annual budget approved each year for 3950 which revenue is not provided by other sources. The aggregate 3951 annual assessment amount must be allocated upon a formula to be 3952 determined by the Interstate Commission, which shall adopt a 3953 rule binding upon all member states. 3954 (2) The Interstate Commission may not incur obligations of 3955 any kind before securing the funds adequate to meet the same. 3956 (3) The Interstate Commission may not pledge the credit of 3957 any of the member states, except by, and with the authority of, 3958 the member state. 3959 (4) The Interstate Commission is subject to an annual 3960 financial audit conducted by a certified or licensed public 3961 accountant, and the report of the audit must be included in the 3962 annual report of the Interstate Commission. 3963 SECTION 13 3965 ORGANIZATION AND OPERATION OF THE INTERSTATE COMMISSION 3966 (1) The Interstate Commission shall, by a majority of 3967 commissioners present and voting, adopt bylaws to govern its 3968 conduct as may be necessary or appropriate to carry out the 3969 purposes of the compact within 12 months after the first 3970 Interstate Commission meeting. 3971 (2) The Interstate Commission shall elect or appoint 3972 annually from among its commissioners a chairperson, a vice 3973 ENROLLED 2024 Legislature CS for SB 7016, 1st Engrossed 20247016er Page 138 of 232 CODING: Words stricken are deletions; words underlined are additions. chairperson, and a treasurer, each of whom shall have such 3974 authority and duties as may be specified in the bylaws. RULEMAKING FUNCTIONS OF THE INTERSTATE COMMISSION 4032 (1) The Interstate Commission shall adopt reasonable rules 4033 in order to effectively and efficiently achieve the purposes of 4034 the compact. However, in the event the Interstate Commission 4035 exercises its rulemaking authority in a manner that is beyond 4036 the scope of the purposes of the compact, or the powers granted 4037 hereunder, then such an action by the Interstate Commission is 4038 invalid and has no force or effect. 4039 (2) Rules deemed appropriate for the operations of the 4040 Interstate Commission must be made pursuant to a rulemaking 4041 process that substantially conforms to the “Model State 4042 Administrative Procedure Act” of 2010, and subsequent amendments 4043 thereto. 4044 (3) Not later than 30 days after a rule is adopted, any 4045 person may file a petition for judicial review of the rule in 4046 the United States District Court for the District of Columbia or 4047 the federal district where the Interstate Commission has its 4048 principal offices, provided that the filing of such a petition 4049 does not stay or otherwise prevent the rule from becoming 4050 effective unless the court finds that the petitioner has a 4051 substantial likelihood of success. The court must give deference 4052 to the actions of the Interstate Commission consistent with 4053 applicable law and may not find the rule to be unlawful if the 4054 rule represents a reasonable exercise of the authority granted 4055 to the Interstate Commission. 4056 SECTION 15 4058 OVERSIGHT OF INTERSTATE COMPACT 4059 (1) The executive, legislative, and judicial branches of 4060 ENROLLED 2024 Legislature CS for SB 7016, 1st Engrossed 20247016er Page 141 of 232 CODING: Words stricken are deletions; words underlined are additions. state government in each member state shall enforce the compact 4061 and shall take all actions necessary and appropriate to 4062 effectuate the compact’s purposes and intent. The compact and 4063 the rules adopted hereunder shall have standing as statutory law 4064 but do not override existing state authority to regulate the 4065 practice of medicine. 4066 (2) All courts shall take judicial notice of the compact 4067 and the rules in any judicial or administrative proceeding in a 4068 member state pertaining to the subject matter of the compact 4069 which may affect the powers, responsibilities, or actions of the 4070 Interstate Commission. 4071 (3) The Interstate Commission is entitled to receive all 4072 service of process in any such proceeding and shall have 4073 standing to intervene in the proceeding for all purposes. 4074 Failure to provide service of process to the Interstate 4075 Commission shall render a judgment or order void as to the 4076 Interstate Commission, the compact, or adopted rules, as 4077 applicable. 4078 SECTION 16 4080 ENFORCEMENT OF INTERSTATE COMPACT 4081 (1) The Interstate Commission, in the reasonable exercise 4082 of its discretion, shall enforce the provisions and rules of the 4083 compact. 4084 (2) The Interstate Commission may, by majority vote of the 4085 commissioners, initiate legal action in the United States 4086 District Court for the District of Columbia, or, at the 4087 discretion of the Interstate Commission, in the federal district 4088 where the Interstate Commission has its principal offices, to 4089 ENROLLED 2024 Legislature CS for SB 7016, 1st Engrossed 20247016er Page 142 of 232 CODING: Words stricken are deletions; words underlined are additions. enforce compliance with the compact and its adopted rules and 4090 bylaws against a member state in default. The Interstate Commission shall specify the 4115 conditions by which the defaulting state must cure its default; 4116 and 4117 (b) Provide remedial training and specific technical 4118 ENROLLED 2024 Legislature CS for SB 7016, 1st Engrossed 20247016er Page 143 of 232 CODING: Words stricken are deletions; words underlined are additions. assistance regarding the default. 4119 (3) If the defaulting state fails to cure the default, the 4120 defaulting state may be terminated from the compact upon an 4121 affirmative vote of a majority of the commissioners and all 4122 rights, privileges, and benefits conferred by the compact 4123 terminate on the effective date of the termination. A cure of 4124 the default does not relieve the offending state of obligations 4125 or liabilities incurred during the period of the default. 4126 (4) Termination of membership in the compact must be 4127 imposed only after all other means of securing compliance have 4128 been exhausted. Notice of intent to terminate must be given by 4129 the Interstate Commission to the governor, the majority and 4130 minority leaders of the defaulting state’s legislature, and each 4131 of the member states. 4132 (5) The Interstate Commission shall establish rules and 4133 procedures to address licenses and physicians that are 4134 materially impacted by the termination of a member state, or the 4135 withdrawal of a member state. 4136 (6) The member state which has been terminated is 4137 responsible for all dues, obligations, and liabilities incurred 4138 through the effective date of termination, including 4139 obligations, the performance of which extends beyond the 4140 effective date of termination. 4141 (7) The Interstate Commission shall not bear any costs 4142 relating to any state that has been found to be in default or 4143 which has been terminated from the compact, unless otherwise 4144 mutually agreed upon in writing between the Interstate 4145 Commission and the defaulting state. 4146 (8) The defaulting state may appeal the action of the 4147 ENROLLED 2024 Legislature CS for SB 7016, 1st Engrossed 20247016er Page 144 of 232 CODING: Words stricken are deletions; words underlined are additions. Interstate Commission by petitioning the United States District 4148 Court for the District of Columbia or the federal district where 4149 the Interstate Commission has its principal offices. Thereafter, it becomes effective and binding on a 4170 state upon enactment of the compact into law by that state. 4171 (3) The governors of nonmember states, or their designees, 4172 must be invited to participate in the activities of the 4173 Interstate Commission on a nonvoting basis before adoption of 4174 the compact by all states. 4175 (4) The Interstate Commission may propose amendments to the 4176 ENROLLED 2024 Legislature CS for SB 7016, 1st Engrossed 20247016er Page 145 of 232 CODING: Words stricken are deletions; words underlined are additions. compact for enactment by the member states. No amendment becomes 4177 effective and binding upon the Interstate Commission and the 4178 member states unless and until it is enacted into law by 4179 unanimous consent of the member states. 4180 SECTION 20 4182 WITHDRAWAL 4183 (1) Once effective, the compact shall continue in force and 4184 remain binding upon each member state. However, a member state 4185 may withdraw from the compact by specifically repealing the 4186 statute which enacted the compact into law. 4187 (2) Withdrawal from the compact must be made by the 4188 enactment of a statute repealing the same, but the withdrawal 4189 shall not take effect until 1 year after the effective date of 4190 such statute and until written notice of the withdrawal has been 4191 given by the withdrawing state to the governor of each other 4192 member state. 4193 (3) The withdrawing state shall immediately notify the 4194 chairperson of the Interstate Commission in writing upon the 4195 introduction of legislation repealing the compact in the 4196 withdrawing state. 4197 (4) The Interstate Commission shall notify the other member 4198 states of the withdrawing state’s intent to withdraw within 60 4199 days after receipt of notice provided under subsection (3). 4200 (5) The withdrawing state is responsible for all dues, 4201 obligations, and liabilities incurred through the effective date 4202 of withdrawal, including obligations, the performance of which 4203 extend beyond the effective date of withdrawal. 4204 (6) Reinstatement following withdrawal of a member state 4205 ENROLLED 2024 Legislature CS for SB 7016, 1st Engrossed 20247016er Page 146 of 232 CODING: Words stricken are deletions; words underlined are additions. shall occur upon the withdrawing state reenacting the compact or 4206 upon such later date as determined by the Interstate Commission. 4207 (7) The Interstate Commission may develop rules to address 4208 the impact of the withdrawal of a member state on licenses 4209 granted in other member states to physicians who designated the 4210 withdrawing member state as the state of principal license. 4211 SECTION 21 4213 DISSOLUTION 4214 (1) The compact shall dissolve effective upon the date of 4215 the withdrawal or default of the member state which reduces the 4216 membership in the compact to one member state. 4217 (2) Upon the dissolution of the compact, the compact 4218 becomes null and void and shall be of no further force or 4219 effect, the business and affairs of the Interstate Commission 4220 must be concluded, and surplus funds of the Interstate 4221 Commission must be distributed in accordance with the bylaws. 4222 SECTION 22 4224 SEVERABILITY AND CONSTRUCTION 4225 (1) The provisions of the compact are severable, and if any 4226 phrase, clause, sentence, or provision is deemed unenforceable, 4227 the remaining provisions of the compact remain enforceable. 4228 (2) The provisions of the compact must be liberally 4229 construed to effectuate its purposes. 4230 (3) The compact may be construed to prohibit the 4231 applicability of other interstate compacts to which the states 4232 are members. 4233 ENROLLED 2024 Legislature CS for SB 7016, 1st Engrossed 20247016er Page 147 of 232 CODING: Words stricken are deletions; SECTION 23 4235 BINDING EFFECT OF COMPACT AND OTHER LAWS 4236 (1) Nothing herein prevents the enforcement of any other 4237 law of a member state which is not inconsistent with the 4238 compact. 4239 (2) All laws in a member state in conflict with the compact 4240 are superseded to the extent of the conflict. 4241 (3) All lawful actions of the Interstate Commission, 4242 including all rules and bylaws adopted by the commission, are 4243 binding upon the member states. 4244 (4) All agreements between the Interstate Commission and 4245 the member states are binding in accordance with their terms. 4246 (5) In the event any provision of the compact exceeds the 4247 constitutional limits imposed on the legislature of any member 4248 state, such provision is ineffective to the extent of the 4249 conflict with the constitutional provision in question in that 4250 member state. 4251 Section 50. Section 468.1335, Florida Statutes, is created 4317 to read: 4318 468.1335 Audiology and Speech-Language Pathology Interstate 4319 Compact.—The Audiology and Speech-Language Pathology Interstate 4320 Compact is hereby enacted into law and entered into by this 4321 ENROLLED 2024 Legislature CS for SB 7016, 1st Engrossed 20247016er Page 150 of 232 CODING: Words stricken are deletions; words underlined are additions. state with all other states legally joining therein in the form 4322 substantially as follows: 4323 ARTICLE I 4325 PURPOSE 4326 (1) The purpose of the compact is to facilitate the 4327 interstate practice of audiology and speech-language pathology 4328 with the goal of improving public access to audiology and 4329 speech-language pathology services. 4330 (2) The practice of audiology and speech-language pathology 4331 occurs in the state where the patient, client, or student is 4332 located at the time the services are provided. 4333 (3) The compact preserves the regulatory authority of 4334 states to protect the public health and safety through the 4335 current system of state licensure. 4336 (4) The compact is designed to achieve all of the following 4337 objectives: 4338 (a) Increase public access to audiology and speech-language 4339 pathology services by providing for the mutual recognition of 4340 other member state licenses. 4341 (b) Enhance the states’ abilities to protect public health 4342 and safety. 4343 (c) Encourage the cooperation of member states in 4344 regulating multistate audiology and speech-language pathology 4345 practices. 4346 (d) Support spouses of relocating active duty military 4347 personnel. 4348 (e) Enhance the exchange of licensure, investigative, and 4349 disciplinary information between member states. 4350 ENROLLED 2024 Legislature CS for SB 7016, 1st Engrossed 20247016er Page 151 of 232 CODING: Words stricken are deletions; words underlined are additions. (f) Allow a remote state to hold a licensee with compact 4351 privilege in that state accountable to that state’s practice 4352 standards. 4353 (g) Allow for the use of telehealth technology to 4354 facilitate increased access to audiology and speech-language 4355 pathology services. 4356 ARTICLE II 4358 DEFINITIONS 4359 As used in the compact, the term: 4360 (1) “Active duty military” means full-time duty status in 4361 the active uniformed service of the United States, including 4362 members of the National Guard and Reserve on active duty orders 4363 pursuant to 10 U.S.C. chapters 1209 and 1211. 4364 (2) “Adverse action” means any administrative, civil, 4365 equitable, or criminal action permitted by a state’s laws which 4366 is imposed by a licensing board against a licensee, including 4367 actions against an individual’s license or privilege to 4368 practice, such as revocation, suspension, probation, monitoring 4369 of the licensee, or restriction on the licensee’s practice. 4370 (3) “Alternative program” means a nondisciplinary 4371 monitoring process approved by an audiology licensing board or a 4372 speech-language pathology licensing board to address impaired 4373 licensees. 4374 (4) “Audiologist” means an individual who is licensed by a 4375 state to practice audiology. 4376 (5) “Audiology” means the care and services provided by a 4377 licensed audiologist as provided in the member state’s rules and 4378 regulations. 4379 ENROLLED 2024 Legislature CS for SB 7016, 1st Engrossed 20247016er Page 152 of 232 CODING: Words stricken are deletions; words underlined are additions. (2) For the purposes of compact privilege, an audiologist 4612 or speech-language pathologist may hold only one home state 4613 license at a time. 4614 (3) Except as provided in Article VI, if an audiologist or 4615 speech-language pathologist changes his or her primary state of 4616 residence by moving between two member states, the audiologist 4617 or speech-language pathologist must apply for licensure in the 4618 new home state, and the license issued by the prior home state 4619 shall be deactivated in accordance with applicable rules adopted 4620 by the commission. 4621 (4) The audiologist or speech-language pathologist may 4622 apply for licensure in advance of a change in his or her primary 4623 state of residence. 4624 (5) A license may not be issued by the new home state until 4625 the audiologist or speech-language pathologist provides 4626 satisfactory evidence of a change in his or her primary state of 4627 residence to the new home state and satisfies all applicable 4628 requirements to obtain a license from the new home state. 4629 (6) If an audiologist or speech-language pathologist 4630 changes his or her primary state of residence by moving from a 4631 member state to a nonmember state, the license issued by the 4632 prior home state shall convert to a single-state license, valid 4633 only in the former home state. 4634 (7) Compact privilege is valid until the expiration date of 4635 the home state license. words underlined are additions. shall function within the laws and regulations of the remote 4641 state. 4642 (9) A remote state may, in accordance with due process and 4643 state law, remove a licensee’s compact privilege in the remote 4644 state for a specific period of time, impose fines, or take any 4645 other necessary actions to protect the health and safety of its 4646 residents. 4647 (10) If a home state license is encumbered, the licensee 4648 shall lose compact privilege in all remote states until both of 4649 the following occur: 4650 (a) The home state license is no longer encumbered. 4651 (b) Two years have lapsed from the date of the adverse 4652 action. 4653 (11) Once an encumbered license in the home state is 4654 restored to good standing, the licensee must meet the 4655 requirements of subsection (1) to obtain compact privilege in 4656 any remote state. 4657 (12) Once the requirements of subsection (10) have been 4658 met, the licensee must meet the requirements in subsection (1) 4659 to obtain compact privilege in a remote state. 4660 ARTICLE V 4662 COMPACT PRIVILEGE TO PRACTICE TELEHEALTH 4663 Member states shall recognize the right of an audiologist 4664 or speech-language pathologist, licensed by a home state in 4665 accordance with Article III and under rules adopted by the 4666 commission, to practice audiology or speech-language pathology 4667 in any member state through the use of telehealth under 4668 privilege to practice as provided in the compact and rules 4669 ENROLLED 2024 Legislature CS for SB 7016, 1st Engrossed 20247016er Page 162 of 232 CODING: Words stricken are deletions; The commission may pursue 5108 any other remedies available under federal or state law. 5109 ARTICLE XII 5111 EFFECTIVE DATE, WITHDRAWAL, AND AMENDMENT 5112 (1) The compact becomes effective and binding on the date 5113 of legislative enactment of the compact by no fewer than 10 5114 member states. The provisions, which become effective at that 5115 time, shall be limited to the powers granted to the commission 5116 relating to assembly and the adoption of rules. Thereafter, the 5117 commission shall meet and exercise rulemaking powers as 5118 necessary to implement and administer the compact. 5119 (2) Any state that joins the compact subsequent to the 5120 commission’s initial adoption of the rules is subject to the 5121 rules as they exist on the date on which the compact becomes law 5122 in that state. Any rule that has been previously adopted by the 5123 commission has the full force and effect of law on the day the 5124 compact becomes law in that state. 5125 (3) A member state may withdraw from the compact by 5126 enacting a statute repealing the compact. 5127 (a) A member state’s withdrawal does not take effect until 5128 6 months after enactment of the repealing statute. 5129 (b) Withdrawal does not affect the continuing requirement 5130 of the withdrawing state’s audiology licensing board or speech-5131 language pathology licensing board to comply with the 5132 investigative and adverse action reporting requirements of the 5133 ENROLLED 2024 Legislature CS for SB 7016, 1st Engrossed 20247016er Page 178 of 232 CODING: Words stricken are deletions; words underlined are additions. compact before the effective date of withdrawal. 5134 (4) The compact does not invalidate or prevent any 5135 audiology or speech-language pathology licensure agreement or 5136 other cooperative arrangement between a member state and a 5137 nonmember state which does not conflict with the compact. 5138 (5) The compact may be amended by the member states. An 5139 amendment to the compact does not become effective and binding 5140 upon any member state until it is enacted into the laws of all 5141 member states. 5142 ARTICLE XIII 5144 CONSTRUCTION AND SEVERABILITY 5145 The compact must be liberally construed so as to effectuate 5146 its purposes. The provisions of the compact are severable and if 5147 any phrase, clause, sentence, or provision of the compact is 5148 declared to be contrary to the constitution of any member state 5149 or of the United States or the applicability thereof to any 5150 government, agency, person, or circumstance is held invalid, the 5151 validity of the remainder of the compact and the applicability 5152 thereof to any government, agency, person, or circumstance is 5153 not affected. If the compact is held contrary to the 5154 constitution of any member state, it shall remain in full force 5155 and effect as to the remaining member states and in full force 5156 and effect as to the member state affected as to all severable 5157 matters. 5158 ARTICLE XIV 5160 BINDING EFFECT OF COMPACT AND OTHER LAWS 5161 (1) This compact does not prevent the enforcement of any 5162 ENROLLED 2024 Legislature CS for SB 7016, 1st Engrossed 20247016er Page 179 of 232 CODING: Words stricken are deletions; words underlined are additions. other law of a member state which is not inconsistent with the 5163 compact. 5164 (2) All laws of a member state in conflict with the compact 5165 are superseded to the extent of the conflict. 5166 (3) All lawful actions of the commission, including all 5167 rules and bylaws adopted by the commission, are binding upon the 5168 member states. 5169 (4) All agreements between the commission and the member 5170 states are binding in accordance with their terms. 5171 (5) In the event any provision of the compact exceeds the 5172 constitutional limits imposed on the legislature of any member 5173 state, the provision is ineffective to the extent of the 5174 conflict with the constitutional provision in question in that 5175 member state. 5176 Section 56. Section 486.112, Florida Statutes, is created 5359 to read: 5360 486.112 Physical Therapy Licensure Compact.—The Physical 5361 Therapy Licensure Compact is hereby enacted into law and entered 5362 into by this state with all other jurisdictions legally joining 5363 therein in the form substantially as follows: 5364 ENROLLED 2024 Legislature CS for SB 7016, 1st Engrossed 20247016er Page 186 of 232 CODING: Words stricken are deletions; words underlined are additions. ARTICLE I 5366 PURPOSE AND OBJECTIVES 5367 (1) The purpose of the compact is to facilitate interstate 5368 practice of physical therapy with the goal of improving public 5369 access to physical therapy services. The compact preserves the 5370 regulatory authority of member states to protect public health 5371 and safety through their current systems of state licensure. For 5372 purposes of state regulation under the compact, the practice of 5373 physical therapy is deemed to have occurred in the state where 5374 the patient is located at the time physical therapy is provided 5375 to the patient. 5376 (2) The compact is designed to achieve all of the following 5377 objectives: 5378 (a) Increase public access to physical therapy services by 5379 providing for the mutual recognition of other member state 5380 licenses. 5381 (b) Enhance the states’ ability to protect the public’s 5382 health and safety. 5383 (c) Encourage the cooperation of member states in 5384 regulating multistate physical therapy practice. 5385 (d) Support spouses of relocating military members. 5386 (e) Enhance the exchange of licensure, investigative, and 5387 disciplinary information between member states. 5388 (f) Allow a remote state to hold a provider of services 5389 with a compact privilege in that state accountable to that 5390 state’s practice standards. 5391 ARTICLE II 5393 DEFINITIONS 5394 ENROLLED 2024 Legislature CS for SB 7016, 1st Engrossed 20247016er Page 187 of 232 CODING: Words stricken are deletions; words underlined are additions. As used in the compact, and except as otherwise provided, 5395 the term: 5396 (1)“Active duty military” means full-time duty status in 5397 the active uniformed service of the United States, including 5398 members of the National Guard and Reserve on active duty orders 5399 pursuant to 10 U.S.C. chapter 1209 or chapter 1211. 5400 (2) “Adverse action” means disciplinary action taken by a 5401 physical therapy licensing board based upon misconduct, 5402 unacceptable performance, or a combination of both. 5403 (3) “Alternative program” means a nondisciplinary 5404 monitoring or practice remediation process approved by a state’s 5405 physical therapy licensing board. The term includes, but is not 5406 limited to, programs that address substance abuse issues. 5407 (4) “Compact privilege” means the authorization granted by 5408 a remote state to allow a licensee from another member state to 5409 practice as a physical therapist or physical therapist assistant 5410 in the remote state under its laws and rules. 5411 (5) “Continuing competence” means a requirement, as a 5412 condition of license renewal, to provide evidence of 5413 participation in, and completion of, educational and 5414 professional activities relevant to the practice of physical 5415 therapy. 5416 (6) “Data system” means the coordinated database and 5417 reporting system created by the Physical Therapy Compact 5418 Commission for the exchange of information between member states 5419 relating to licensees or applicants under the compact, including 5420 identifying information, licensure data, investigative 5421 information, adverse actions, nonconfidential information 5422 related to alternative program participation, any denials of 5423 ENROLLED 2024 Legislature CS for SB 7016, 1st Engrossed 20247016er Page 188 of 232 CODING: Words stricken are deletions; words underlined are additions. applications for licensure, and other information as specified 5424 by commission rule. 5425 (7) “Encumbered license” means a license that a physical 5426 therapy licensing board has limited in any way. 5427 (8) “Executive board” means a group of directors elected or 5428 appointed to act on behalf of, and within the powers granted to 5429 them by, the commission. 5430 (9) “Home state” means the member state that is the 5431 licensee’s primary state of residence. 5432 (10) “Investigative information” means information, 5433 records, and documents received or generated by a physical 5434 therapy licensing board pursuant to an investigation. 5435 (11) “Jurisprudence requirement” means the assessment of an 5436 individual’s knowledge of the laws and rules governing the 5437 practice of physical therapy in a specific state. 5438 (12) “Licensee” means an individual who currently holds an 5439 authorization from a state to practice as a physical therapist 5440 or physical therapist assistant. 5441 (13) “Member state” means a state that has enacted the 5442 compact. 5443 (14) “Physical therapist” means an individual licensed by a 5444 state to practice physical therapy. 5445 (15) “Physical therapist assistant” means an individual 5446 licensed by a state to assist a physical therapist in specified 5447 areas of physical therapy. 5448 (16) “Physical therapy” or “the practice of physical 5449 therapy” means the care and services provided by or under the 5450 direction and supervision of a licensed physical therapist. 5451 (17) “Physical Therapy Compact Commission” or “commission” 5452 ENROLLED 2024 Legislature CS for SB 7016, 1st Engrossed 20247016er Page 189 of 232 CODING: Words stricken are deletions; words underlined are additions. means the national administrative body whose membership consists 5453 of all states that have enacted the compact. 5454 (18) “Physical therapy licensing board” means the agency of 5455 a state which is responsible for the licensing and regulation of 5456 physical therapists and physical therapist assistants. 5457 (19) “Remote state” means a member state other than the 5458 home state where a licensee is exercising or seeking to exercise 5459 the compact privilege. 5460 (20) “Rule” means a regulation, principle, or directive 5461 adopted by the commission which has the force of law. 5462 (21) “State” means any state, commonwealth, district, or 5463 territory of the United States of America which regulates the 5464 practice of physical therapy. 5465 ARTICLE III 5467 STATE PARTICIPATION IN THE COMPACT 5468 (1) To participate in the compact, a state must do all of 5469 the following: 5470 (a) Participate fully in the commission’s data system, 5471 including using the commission’s unique identifier, as defined 5472 by commission rule. 5473 (b) Have a mechanism in place for receiving and 5474 investigating complaints about licensees. 5475 (c) Notify the commission, in accordance with the terms of 5476 the compact and rules, of any adverse action or the availability 5477 of investigative information regarding a licensee. 5478 (d) Fully implement a criminal background check 5479 requirement, within a timeframe established by commission rule, 5480 which uses results from the Federal Bureau of Investigation 5481 ENROLLED 2024 Legislature CS for SB 7016, 1st Engrossed 20247016er Page 190 of 232 CODING: Words stricken are deletions; words underlined are additions. record search on criminal background checks to make licensure 5482 decisions in accordance with subsection (2). 5483 (e) Comply with the commission’s rules. 5484 (f) Use a recognized national examination as a requirement 5485 for licensure pursuant to the commission’s rules. 5486 (g) Have continuing competence requirements as a condition 5487 for license renewal. 5488 (2) Upon adoption of the compact, a member state has the 5489 authority to obtain biometric-based information from each 5490 licensee applying for a compact privilege and submit this 5491 information to the Federal Bureau of Investigation for a 5492 criminal background check in accordance with 28 U.S.C. s. 534 5493 and 34 U.S.C. s. 40316. 5494 (3) A member state must grant the compact privilege to a 5495 licensee holding a valid unencumbered license in another member 5496 state in accordance with the terms of the compact and rules. 5497 ARTICLE IV 5499 COMPACT PRIVILEGE 5500 (1) To exercise the compact privilege under the compact, a 5501 licensee must satisfy all of the following conditions: 5502 (a) Hold a license in the home state. 5503 (b) Not have an encumbrance on any state license. 5504 (c) Be eligible for a compact privilege in all member 5505 states in accordance with subsections (4), (7), and (8). 5506 (d) Not have had an adverse action against any license or 5507 compact privilege within the preceding 2 years. 5508 (e) Notify the commission that the licensee is seeking the 5509 compact privilege within a remote state. 5510 ENROLLED 2024 Legislature CS for SB 7016, 1st Engrossed 20247016er Page 191 of 232 CODING: Words stricken are deletions; words underlined are additions. (f) Meet any jurisprudence requirements established by the 5511 remote state in which the licensee is seeking a compact 5512 privilege. 5513 (g) Report to the commission adverse action taken by any 5514 nonmember state within 30 days after the date the adverse action 5515 is taken. 5516 (2) The compact privilege is valid until the expiration 5517 date of the home license. The licensee must continue to meet the 5518 requirements of subsection (1) to maintain the compact privilege 5519 in a remote state. 5520 (3) A licensee providing physical therapy in a remote state 5521 under the compact privilege must comply with the laws and rules 5522 of the remote state. 5523 (4) A licensee providing physical therapy in a remote state 5524 is subject to that state’s regulatory authority. A remote state 5525 may, in accordance with due process and that state’s laws, 5526 remove a licensee’s compact privilege in the remote state for a 5527 specific period of time, impose fines, and take any other 5528 necessary actions to protect the health and safety of its 5529 citizens. The licensee is not eligible for a compact privilege 5530 in any member state until the specific period of time for 5531 removal has ended and all fines are paid. 5532 (5) If a home state license is encumbered, the licensee 5533 loses the compact privilege in any remote state until the 5534 following conditions are met: 5535 (a) The home state license is no longer encumbered. 5536 (b) Two years have elapsed from the date of the adverse 5537 action. 5538 (6) Once an encumbered license in the home state is 5539 ENROLLED 2024 Legislature CS for SB 7016, 1st Engrossed 20247016er Page 192 of 232 CODING: Words stricken are deletions; One ex officio, nonvoting member from the recognized 5755 membership organization of the physical therapy licensing 5756 boards. 5757 (c) The ex officio members shall be selected by their 5758 respective organizations. 5759 (d) The commission may remove any member of the executive 5760 board as provided in its bylaws. 5761 (e) The executive board shall meet at least annually. 5762 (f) The executive board shall do all of the following: 5763 1. Monitor compact compliance of member states and provide 5772 compliance reports to the commission. 5773 6. Establish additional committees as necessary. 5774 7. Perform other duties as provided in the rules or bylaws. 5775 (6) FINANCING OF THE COMMISSION.— 5776 (a) The commission shall pay, or provide for the payment 5777 of, the reasonable expenses of its establishment, organization, 5778 and ongoing activities. 5779 (b) The commission may accept any appropriate revenue 5780 sources, donations, and grants of money, equipment, supplies, 5781 materials, and services. 5782 (c) The commission may levy and collect an annual 5783 assessment from each member state or impose fees on other 5784 parties to cover the cost of the operations and activities of 5785 the commission and its staff. Such assessments and fees must 5786 total to an amount sufficient to cover the commission’s annual 5787 budget as approved each year for which revenue is not provided 5788 by other sources. The aggregate annual assessment amount must be 5789 allocated based upon a formula to be determined by the 5790 commission, which shall adopt a rule binding upon all member 5791 states. 5792 (d) The commission may not incur obligations of any kind 5793 before securing the funds adequate to meet such obligations; nor 5794 may the commission pledge the credit of any of the member 5795 states, except by and with the authority of the member state. 5796 (e) The commission shall keep accurate accounts of all 5797 receipts and disbursements. The receipts and disbursements of 5798 the commission are subject to the audit and accounting 5799 procedures established under its bylaws. However, all receipts 5800 ENROLLED 2024 Legislature CS for SB 7016, 1st Engrossed 20247016er Page 201 of 232 CODING: Words stricken are deletions; words underlined are additions. and disbursements of funds handled by the commission must be 5801 audited yearly by a certified or licensed public accountant, and 5802 the report of the audit must be included in and become part of 5803 the annual report of the commission. 5804 (7) QUALIFIED IMMUNITY, DEFENSE, AND INDEMNIFICATION.— 5805 (a) The members, officers, executive director, employees, 5806 and representatives of the commission are immune from suit and 5807 liability, whether personally or in their official capacity, for 5808 any claim for damage to or loss of property or personal injury 5809 or other civil liability caused by or arising out of any actual 5810 or alleged act, error, or omission that occurred, or that the 5811 person against whom the claim is made had a reasonable basis for 5812 believing occurred, within the scope of commission employment, 5813 duties, or responsibilities. However, this paragraph may not be 5814 construed to protect any such person from suit or liability for 5815 any damage, loss, injury, or liability caused by the 5816 intentional, willful, or wanton misconduct of that person. 5817 (b) The commission shall defend any member, officer, 5818 executive director, employee, or representative of the 5819 commission in any civil action seeking to impose liability 5820 arising out of any actual or alleged act, error, or omission 5821 that occurred within the scope of commission employment, duties, 5822 or responsibilities, or that the person against whom the claim 5823 is made had a reasonable basis for believing occurred within the 5824 scope of commission employment, duties, or responsibilities. 5825 However, this subsection may not be construed to prohibit any 5826 member, officer, executive director, employee, or representative 5827 of the commission from retaining his or her own counsel or to 5828 require the commission to defend such person if the actual or 5829 ENROLLED 2024 Legislature CS for SB 7016, 1st Engrossed 20247016er Page 202 of 232 CODING: Words stricken are deletions; words underlined are additions. alleged act, error, or omission resulted from that person’s 5830 intentional, willful, or wanton misconduct. 5831 (c) The commission shall indemnify and hold harmless any 5832 member, officer, executive director, employee, or representative 5833 of the commission for the amount of any settlement or judgment 5834 obtained against that person arising out of any actual or 5835 alleged act, error, or omission that occurred within the scope 5836 of commission employment, duties, or responsibilities, or that 5837 such person had a reasonable basis for believing occurred within 5838 the scope of commission employment, duties, or responsibilities, 5839 provided that the actual or alleged act, error, or omission did 5840 not result from the intentional, willful, or wanton misconduct 5841 of that person. 5842 ARTICLE VIII 5844 DATA SYSTEM 5845 (1) The commission shall provide for the development, 5846 maintenance, and use of a coordinated database and reporting 5847 system containing licensure, adverse action, and investigative 5848 information on all licensees in member states. 5849 (2) Notwithstanding any other provision of state law to the 5850 contrary, a member state shall submit a uniform data set to the 5851 data system on all individuals to whom the compact is applicable 5852 as required by the rules of the commission, which data set must 5853 include all of the following: 5854 (a) Identifying information. 5855 (b) Licensure data. 5856 (c) Investigative information. 5857 (d) Adverse actions against a license or compact privilege. 5858 ENROLLED 2024 Legislature CS for SB 7016, 1st Engrossed 20247016er Page 203 of 232 CODING: Words stricken are deletions; words underlined are additions. (e) Nonconfidential information related to alternative 5859 program participation. 5860 (f) Any denial of application for licensure and the reason 5861 for such denial. 5862 (g) Other information that may facilitate the 5863 administration of the compact, as determined by the rules of the 5864 commission. 5865 (3) Investigative information in the system pertaining to a 5866 licensee in any member state must be available only to other 5867 member states. 5868 (4) The commission shall promptly notify all member states 5869 of any adverse action taken against a licensee or an individual 5870 applying for a license in a member state. Adverse action 5871 information pertaining to a licensee in any member state must be 5872 available to all other member states. 5873 (5) Member states contributing information to the data 5874 system may designate information that may not be shared with the 5875 public without the express permission of the contributing state. 5876 (6) Any information submitted to the data system which is 5877 subsequently required to be expunged by the laws of the member 5878 state contributing the information must be removed from the data 5879 system. 5880 ARTICLE IX 5882 RULEMAKING 5883 (1) The commission shall exercise its rulemaking powers 5884 pursuant to the criteria set forth in this article and the rules 5885 adopted thereunder. Rules and amendments become binding as of 5886 the date specified in each rule or amendment. 5887 ENROLLED 2024 Legislature CS for SB 7016, 1st Engrossed 20247016er Page 204 of 232 CODING: Words stricken are deletions; words underlined are additions. (2) If a majority of the legislatures of the member states 5888 rejects a rule by enactment of a statute or resolution in the 5889 same manner used to adopt the compact within 4 years after the 5890 date of adoption of the rule, such rule does not have further 5891 force and effect in any member state. 5892 (3) Rules or amendments to the rules must be adopted at a 5893 regular or special meeting of the commission. 5894 (4) Before adoption of a final rule by the commission, and 5895 at least 30 days before the meeting at which the rule will be 5896 considered and voted upon, the commission must file a notice of 5897 proposed rulemaking on all of the following: 5898 (a) The website of the commission or another publicly 5899 accessible platform. 5900 (b) The website of each member state physical therapy 5901 licensing board or another publicly accessible platform or the 5902 publication in which each state would otherwise publish proposed 5903 rules. 5904 (5) The notice of proposed rulemaking must include all of 5905 the following: 5906 (a) The proposed date, time, and location of the meeting in 5907 which the rule or amendment will be considered and voted upon. 5908 (b) The text of the proposed rule or amendment and the 5909 reason for the proposed rule. 5910 (c) A request for comments on the proposed rule or 5911 amendment from any interested person. 5912 (d) The manner in which interested persons may submit 5913 notice to the commission of their intention to attend the public 5914 hearing and any written comments. 5915 (6) Before adoption of a proposed rule or amendment, the 5916 ENROLLED 2024 Legislature CS for SB 7016, 1st Engrossed 20247016er Page 205 of 232 CODING: Words stricken are deletions; words underlined are additions. commission must allow persons to submit written data, facts, 5917 opinions, and arguments, which must be made available to the 5918 public. 5919 (7) The commission must grant an opportunity for a public 5920 hearing before it adopts a rule or an amendment if a hearing is 5921 requested by any of the following: 5922 (a) At least 25 persons. 5923 (b) A state or federal governmental subdivision or agency. 5924 (c) An association having at least 25 members. 5925 (8) If a scheduled public hearing is held on the proposed 5926 rule or amendment, the commission must publish the date, time, 5927 and location of the hearing. If the hearing is held through 5928 electronic means, the commission must publish the mechanism for 5929 access to the electronic hearing. 5930 (a) All persons wishing to be heard at the hearing must 5931 notify the executive director of the commission or another 5932 designated member in writing of their desire to appear and 5933 testify at the hearing at least 5 business days before the 5934 scheduled date of the hearing. 5935 (b) Hearings must be conducted in a manner providing each 5936 person who wishes to comment a fair and reasonable opportunity 5937 to comment orally or in writing. 5938 (c) All hearings must be recorded. A copy of the recording 5939 must be made available on request. 5940 (d) This article may not be construed to require a separate 5941 hearing on each rule. Rules may be grouped for the convenience 5942 of the commission at hearings required by this article. 5943 (9) Following the scheduled hearing date, or by the close 5944 of business on the scheduled hearing date if the hearing was not 5945 ENROLLED 2024 Legislature CS for SB 7016, 1st Engrossed 20247016er Page 206 of 232 CODING: Words stricken are deletions; words underlined are additions. held, the commission shall consider all written and oral 5946 comments received. 5947 (10) If no written notice of intent to attend the public 5948 hearing by interested parties is received, the commission may 5949 proceed with adoption of the proposed rule without a public 5950 hearing. 5951 (11) The commission shall, by majority vote of all members, 5952 take final action on the proposed rule and shall determine the 5953 effective date of the rule, if any, based on the rulemaking 5954 record and the full text of the rule. 5955 (12) Upon determination that an emergency exists, the 5956 commission may consider and adopt an emergency rule without 5957 prior notice, opportunity for comment, or hearing, provided that 5958 the usual rulemaking procedures provided in the compact and in 5959 this article are retroactively applied to the rule as soon as 5960 reasonably possible, in no event later than 90 days after the 5961 effective date of the rule. For the purposes of this subsection, 5962 an emergency rule is one that must be adopted immediately in 5963 order to do any of the following: 5964 (a) Meet an imminent threat to public health, safety, or 5965 welfare. 5966 (b) Prevent a loss of commission or member state funds. 5967 (c) Meet a deadline for the adoption of an administrative 5968 rule established by federal law or rule. 5969 (d) Protect public health and safety. 5970 (13) The commission or an authorized committee of the 5971 commission may direct revisions to a previously adopted rule or 5972 amendment for purposes of correcting typographical errors, 5973 errors in format, errors in consistency, or grammatical errors. 5974 ENROLLED 2024 Legislature CS for SB 7016, 1st Engrossed 20247016er Page 207 of 232 CODING: Words stricken are deletions; words underlined are additions. Public notice of any revisions must be posted on the website of 5975 the commission. The revision is subject to challenge by any 5976 person for a period of 30 days after posting. The revision may 5977 be challenged only on grounds that the revision results in a 5978 material change to a rule. A challenge must be made in writing 5979 and delivered to the chair of the commission before the end of 5980 the notice period. If a challenge is not made, the revision 5981 takes effect without further action. If the revision is 5982 challenged, the revision may not take effect without the 5983 approval of the commission. 5984 ARTICLE X 5986 OVERSIGHT, DISPUTE RESOLUTION, AND ENFORCEMENT 5987 (1) OVERSIGHT.— 5988 (a) The executive, legislative, and judicial branches of 5989 state government in each member state shall enforce the compact 5990 and take all actions necessary and appropriate to carry out the 5991 compact’s purposes and intent. The provisions of the compact and 5992 the rules adopted pursuant thereto shall have standing as 5993 statutory law. 5994 (b) All courts shall take judicial notice of the compact 5995 and the rules in any judicial or administrative proceeding in a 5996 member state pertaining to the subject matter of the compact 5997 which may affect the powers, responsibilities, or actions of the 5998 commission. 5999 (c) The commission is entitled to receive service of 6000 process in any such proceeding and has standing to intervene in 6001 such a proceeding for all purposes. Failure to provide service 6002 of process to the commission renders a judgment or an order void 6003 ENROLLED 2024 Legislature CS for SB 7016, 1st Engrossed 20247016er Page 208 of 232 CODING: Words stricken are deletions; words underlined are additions. as to the commission, the compact, or the adopted rules. 6004 (2) DEFAULT, TECHNICAL ASSISTANCE, AND TERMINATION.— 6005 (a) If the commission determines that a member state has 6006 defaulted in the performance of its obligations or 6007 responsibilities under the compact or the adopted rules, the 6008 commission must do all of the following: 6009 1. Provide written notice to the defaulting state and other 6010 member states of the nature of the default, the proposed means 6011 of curing the default, and any other action to be taken by the 6012 commission. 6013 2. Provide remedial training and specific technical 6014 assistance regarding the default. 6015 (b) If a state in default fails to cure the default, the 6016 defaulting state may be terminated from the compact upon an 6017 affirmative vote of a majority of the member states, and all 6018 rights, privileges, and benefits conferred by the compact may be 6019 terminated on the effective date of termination. A cure of the 6020 default does not relieve the offending state of obligations or 6021 liabilities incurred during the period of default. 6022 (c) Termination of membership in the compact may be imposed 6023 only after all other means of securing compliance have been 6024 exhausted. The commission shall give notice of intent to suspend 6025 or terminate a defaulting member state to the governor and 6026 majority and minority leaders of the defaulting state’s 6027 legislature and to each of the member states. 6028 (d) A state that has been terminated from the compact is 6029 responsible for all assessments, obligations, and liabilities 6030 incurred through the effective date of termination, including 6031 obligations that extend beyond the effective date of 6032 ENROLLED 2024 Legislature CS for SB 7016, 1st Engrossed 20247016er Page 209 of 232 CODING: Words stricken are deletions; words underlined are additions. termination. 6033 (e) The commission does not bear any costs related to a 6034 state that is found to be in default or that has been terminated 6035 from the compact, unless agreed upon in writing between the 6036 commission and the defaulting state. 6037 (f) The defaulting state may appeal the action of the 6038 commission by petitioning the U.S. District Court for the 6039 District of Columbia or the federal district where the 6040 commission has its principal offices. The prevailing member 6041 shall be awarded all costs of such litigation, including 6042 reasonable attorney fees. 6043 (3) DISPUTE RESOLUTION.— 6044 (a) Upon request by a member state, the commission must 6045 attempt to resolve disputes related to the compact which arise 6046 among member states and between member and nonmember states. 6047 (b) The commission shall adopt a rule providing for both 6048 mediation and binding dispute resolution for disputes as 6049 appropriate. 6050 (4) ENFORCEMENT.— 6051 (a) The commission, in the reasonable exercise of its 6052 discretion, shall enforce the compact and the commission’s 6053 rules. 6054 (b) By majority vote, the commission may initiate legal 6055 action in the United States District Court for the District of 6056 Columbia or the federal district where the commission has its 6057 principal offices against a member state in default to enforce 6058 compliance with the provisions of the compact and its adopted 6059 rules and bylaws. The relief sought may include both injunctive 6060 relief and damages. In the event judicial enforcement is 6061 ENROLLED 2024 Legislature CS for SB 7016, 1st Engrossed 20247016er Page 210 of 232 CODING: Words stricken are deletions; words underlined are additions. necessary, the prevailing member shall be awarded all costs of 6062 such litigation, including reasonable attorney fees. 6063 (c) The remedies under this article are not the exclusive 6064 remedies of the commission. The commission may pursue any other 6065 remedies available under federal or state law. 6066 ARTICLE XI 6068 DATE OF IMPLEMENTATION OF THE PHYSICAL THERAPY COMPACT AND 6069 ASSOCIATED RULES; WITHDRAWAL; AND AMENDMENTS 6070 (1) The compact becomes effective on the date that the 6071 compact statute is enacted into law in the tenth member state. 6072 The provisions that become effective at that time are limited to 6073 the powers granted to the commission relating to assembly and 6074 the adoption of rules. Thereafter, the commission shall meet and 6075 exercise rulemaking powers necessary for the implementation and 6076 administration of the compact. 6077 (2) Any state that joins the compact subsequent to the 6078 commission’s initial adoption of the rules is subject to the 6079 rules as they exist on the date that the compact becomes law in 6080 that state. Any rule that has been previously adopted by the 6081 commission has the full force and effect of law on the day the 6082 compact becomes law in that state. 6083 (3) Any member state may withdraw from the compact by 6084 enacting a statute repealing the same. 6085 (a) A member state’s withdrawal does not take effect until 6086 6 months after enactment of the repealing statute. 6087 (b) Withdrawal does not affect the continuing requirement 6088 of the withdrawing state’s physical therapy licensing board to 6089 comply with the investigative and adverse action reporting 6090 ENROLLED 2024 Legislature CS for SB 7016, 1st Engrossed 20247016er Page 211 of 232 CODING: Words stricken are deletions; words underlined are additions. requirements of this act before the effective date of 6091 withdrawal. 6092 (4) The compact may not be construed to invalidate or 6093 prevent any physical therapy licensure agreement or other 6094 cooperative arrangement between a member state and a nonmember 6095 state which does not conflict with the provisions of the 6096 compact. 6097 (5) The compact may be amended by the member states. An 6098 amendment to the compact does not become effective and binding 6099 upon any member state until it is enacted into the laws of all 6100 member states. 6101 ARTICLE XII 6103 CONSTRUCTION AND SEVERABILITY 6104 The compact must be liberally construed so as to carry out 6105 the purposes thereof. The provisions of the compact are 6106 severable, and if any phrase, clause, sentence, or provision of 6107 the compact is declared to be contrary to the constitution of 6108 any member state or of the United States or the applicability 6109 thereof to any government, agency, person, or circumstance is 6110 held invalid, the validity of the remainder of the compact and 6111 the applicability thereof to any government, agency, person, or 6112 circumstance is not affected thereby. If the compact is held 6113 contrary to the constitution of any member state, the compact 6114 remains in full force and effect as to the remaining member 6115 states and in full force and effect as to the member state 6116 affected as to all severable matters. 6117 Section 61.
